\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0})),\\
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0})).\end{array}

Lemma 3.20
.

Using (
82
) and (
3.4
), the source and target maps
{\sf s},{\sf t}\colon{\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\rightrightarrows{\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))
are determined by the super algebra maps

\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{1|1}))\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{\leq 0}^{1|1}))

{\sf s}^{*}(\alpha,\beta)=(\alpha,\beta),\qquad{\sf t}^{*}(\alpha,\beta)=(\alpha-\theta d\alpha,\beta-\theta d\beta),\quad\alpha,\beta\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M),

where we use the canonical inclusions

\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{1|1})),\qquad\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{\leq 0}^{1|1}))

along the constant functions on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}

Proof.

Standard results (e.g.,
[
HKST11
, Lemma 3.8]
) compute the
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
-action on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
in terms of a map on differential forms:

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\simeq C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}))

(86)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\alpha-\theta d\alpha,\quad\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M),\ C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1})\simeq C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})[\theta]

where
d
is the de Rham differential on
\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
. From Lemma
3.18
, the source and target maps in
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
are therefore determined by restrictions of (
86
).
∎

Remark 3.21
.

The formula (
86
) differs from
[
HKST11
, Lemma 3.8]
by a sign; their formula is for the right action of
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
on
\Pi TM_{\mathbb{C}}
, whereas (
86
) is for the left action.

3.5.
Automorphisms of the category of superpaths

Lemma 3.22
.

The maps (
68
), (
69
), (
70
) and (
71
) determine smooth functors

(87)

\displaystyle{\sf Or}\colon{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to{\sf sPath}_{0}(M),

\displaystyle(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(-t,-i\theta,\phi\circ{\sf or}^{-1})

(88)

\displaystyle{\sf RG}\colon{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to{\sf sPath}_{0}(M),

\displaystyle(\mu,t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(\mu^{2}t,\mu\theta,\phi\circ{\sf rg}_{\mu}^{-1})

(89)

\displaystyle{\sf Fl}\colon{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to{\sf sPath}_{0}(M),

\displaystyle(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(t,-\theta,\phi\circ{\sf fl}^{-1})

(90)

\displaystyle{\sf R}\colon{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to\overline{{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)},

\displaystyle(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(\overline{t},\overline{\theta},\overline{\phi}\circ{\sf r})

given by the indicated maps on
S
-points of morphisms. Furthermore, there are equalities of smooth functors

(91)

\displaystyle{\sf Or}\circ{\sf Or}={\sf Fl},\qquad{\sf R}\circ{\sf Or}=\overline{{\sf Fl}}\circ\overline{{\sf Or}}\circ{\sf R}

and
{\sf RG}
gives the structure of a strict
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-action on
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
in the sense that the diagram of super Lie categories strictly commutes

(92)

{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
{{\rm id}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}\times{\sf RG}
m\times{{\rm id}}_{{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)}
{\sf RG}
{\sf RG}

where
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is regarded as a discrete super Lie category and
m\colon{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is multiplication.

Proof.

The formulas (

)-(

) follow from Example
3.11
. The equalities (

) and commutativity (

) follow from (
72
). The real structure (

) is inherited from the real structure on
1|1
-Euclidean supermanifolds in Example
3.11
, together with the canonical real structure on
M
, giving the
\bar{S}
-point of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

\overline{S}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{\overline{S}}\times{\sf r}}\overline{S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}}\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}\overline{M}\simeq M

determined by the image of
\phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\to M
under the conjugation functor on supermanifolds.
∎

We use the notation

{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)

to denote the restriction of the functor
{\sf RG}
along
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.

Corollary 3.23
.

The functors (
87
)-(
89
) are determined by the maps on morphisms induced by the super algebra maps

\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}))\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}))\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}))\oplus\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}))

(93)

\displaystyle{\sf Or}^{*}(\alpha(t,\theta),\beta(t,\theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(i^{|\beta|}\beta(-t,-i\theta),i^{|\alpha|}\alpha(-t,-i\theta)),\quad i=\sqrt{-1}

(94)

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}^{*}(\alpha(t,\theta),\beta(t,\theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mu^{|\alpha|}\alpha(\mu^{2}t,\mu\theta),\mu^{|\beta|}\beta(\mu^{2}t,\mu\theta)

(95)

\displaystyle{\sf Fl}^{*}(\alpha(t,\theta),\beta(t,\theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha(t,-\theta),(-1)^{|\beta|}\beta(t,-\theta))

for
\alpha,\beta\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}))
, where
|\alpha|,|\beta|\in{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading of
\alpha
and
\beta
.

Proof.

This follows from (

)-(

) and standard results (e.g.,
[

, Lemma 3.8]
) that compute the
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-action on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
. The above statements follow by specializing to the subgroups
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
,
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.
∎

3.6.
Superpaths as super loops

Definition 3.24
.

Given an
S
-point
(\ell,\lambda)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}(S)
, define the family of
1|1
-dimensional
super Euclidean circles
as the quotient

(96)

\displaystyle S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}:=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}

for the left
{\mathbb{Z}}
-action on
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
given by

(97)

\displaystyle n\cdot(t,\theta)=(t+n\ell+n\lambda\theta,n\lambda+\theta),\quad n\in{\mathbb{Z}}(S),(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}(S).

Let
S^{1|1}={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the quotient by the standard inclusion
{\mathbb{Z}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
. For any
(\ell,\lambda)
there is an isomorphism

(98)

\displaystyle S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}S\times S^{1|1}

determined by the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-equivariant map

(99)

\displaystyle S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}

\displaystyle(\ell,\lambda,t,\theta)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\ell,\lambda,t(\ell+\lambda\theta),\theta+t\lambda),\qquad(\ell,\lambda)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}(S),\ (t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}(S).

Remark 3.25
.

The
S
-family of subgroups
S\times{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
generated by
(\ell,\lambda)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}(S)
is normal if and only if
\lambda=0
. Hence, the standard super circle
S^{1|1}={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}
inherits a group structure from
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
, but a generic
S
-family of super Euclidean circles
S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}
does not.

Definition 3.26
.

Define the
super Euclidean loop space
as the generalized supermanifold (see Example
A.16
)

\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M):={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}(S^{1|1},M).

We identify an
S
-point of
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
with a map
S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}\to M
given by the composition

(100)

\displaystyle S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}\simeq S\times S^{1|1}\to M

using the isomorphism (
98
). There is a left action of
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
on
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
determined by the diagram

(101)

S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}
S\times S^{1|1}
S^{1|1}_{\ell^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}
S\times S^{1|1}
M
\simeq
f
\simeq
\phi
\phi^{\prime}

where the horizontal arrows are the isomorphism (
98
) and the super Euclidean isometry
f
is inherited from the action of
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
on
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
. The arrow
\phi^{\prime}
is uniquely determined by these isomorphisms and the input map
\phi
. Hence, given
(\ell,\lambda,\phi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}(S)\times{\sf Map}(S^{1|1},M)(S)
and an
S
-point of
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
, the action on
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
outputs
(\ell^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
as in (
101
).

There is an evident
S^{1}
-action on
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
coming from the precomposition action of
S^{1}={\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}<{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}
on
{\sf Map}(S^{1|1},M)
. Since the quotient is given by
S^{1|1}/S^{1}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
, the
S^{1}
-fixed points are

(102)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{1|1}_{0}(M):={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}(S^{1|1},M)=\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M).

We identify an
S
-point of
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}_{0}(M)
with a map
S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}\to M
that factors as

(103)

\displaystyle S^{1|1}_{\ell,\lambda}\simeq S\times S^{1|1}=S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\to M

where the map
p
is induced by the projection
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
. The
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action in (
101
) preserves this factorization condition, and is computed explicitly in
[
BE23a
, §2.3]
.

The equalizer of the source and target maps in
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is

{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\subset{\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\rightrightarrows{\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M)).

Geometrically, this equalizer parameterizes constant superpaths with the same source and target. We obtain nearly constant super loops by restricting to a further subspace.

Lemma 3.27
.

There is a span of inclusions

{\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\hookleftarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{<0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{L}_{0}^{1|1}(M)

that views a constant superpath with the same source and target and nonzero super length as a constant super loop.

4.
Super Euclidean paths as bordisms

The main goal of this section is to construct an internal functor

(104)

\displaystyle\iota\colon{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)

from the super Lie category of constant superpaths in
M
to Stolz and Teichner’s
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
. We further show that (
104
) is compatible with additional structures from Lemma
3.22
: the spin flip, orientation reversal, renormalization group action, and real structure. The arguments are all phrased within the framework of
[
ST11
]
(see §
B
for a review). However, (
104
) is a totally geometric construction that we expect to hold in any reasonable definition of the
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category.

4.1.
The
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category

Definition 4.1
.

Let
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
denote the geometric bordism category associated with the super Euclidean geometry from Example
3.3
. We call
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
the
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
.

We refer to §
B.3
for Stolz and Teichner’s definition of a geometric bordism category associated to a rigid geometry. To briefly summarize,
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
is a category internal to the category of symmetric monoidal stacks with involution determined by the spin flip (see Definition
3.8
and Example
B.26
). Hence, part of the data of
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
are maps of symmetric monoidal stacks with involution

{\sf s},{\sf t}\colon{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\rightrightarrows{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)),\quad{\sf u}\colon{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

{\sf c}\colon{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

giving the source, target, unit and composition maps, where we use the notation
3
3

3

By Lemma
B.16
, the strict fibered product agrees with the weak fibered product.

{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}={\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\times_{{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))}{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)).

for the fibered product relative to the source and target maps, i.e.,
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}
is the stack that classifies pairs of composable bordisms. The full definition of
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
includes further associator and unitor data satisfying certain coherence properties; see §
B.2
.

Structures in the
1|1
-Euclidean geometry discussed in §
3.2
equip the internal category
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
with certain endofunctors.

Lemma 4.2
.

The internal category
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
admits internal endofunctors:

(1)

orientation reversal,
{\sf Or}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
,

(2)

the renormalization group action,
{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
, and

(3)

a real structure,
{\sf R}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
,

where
{\sf Or}
, and
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
are maps of categories internal to symmetric monoidal stacks with involution, whereas the functor
{\sf R}
is an internal functor that covers complex conjugation on supermanifolds.

Proof.

From Definition
3.5
and Example
B.28
, we obtain an orientation reversal functor

(105)

\displaystyle{\sf Or}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M).

Using Definition
3.7
and Example
B.33
, we obtain an internal functor

(106)

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M).

Finally, Definition
3.10
determines an internal functor (see Example
B.29
)

(107)

\displaystyle{\sf R}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)

whose values are given by

(S\leftarrow Y\to M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\sf R}_{0}}}{{\mapsto}}(\overline{S}\leftarrow\overline{Y}\to\overline{M}\simeq M)\qquad(S\leftarrow\Sigma\to M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\sf R}_{1}}}{{\mapsto}}(\overline{S}\leftarrow\overline{\Sigma}\to\overline{M}\simeq M)

where
\overline{Y}\to\overline{S}
and
\overline{\Sigma}\to\overline{S}
have the canonical structure of an
\overline{S}
-family of super Euclidean manifolds. We emphasize that the family parameter changes under the functor
{\sf R}
to the conjugate supermanifold.
∎

A map of smooth manifolds
M\to N
determines an internal functor

1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(N)

see Lemma
B.17
. In particular, the
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
is always an internal category over
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})
, the
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category over a point.

4.2.
Families of bordisms in
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)

Definition
B.15
reviews the construction of the symmetric monoidal stacks with involution
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
and
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
. In this subsection, we use the same notation for the underlying stack, i.e., forgetting the monoidal structure and spin flip. Throughout, the family
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to S
will always have the canonical structure of a
1|1
-Euclidean family. When
M={\rm pt}
, the definitions below specialize to the pictures (
48
), and we use analogous notation to impart this intuition.

Definition 4.3

(Compare
[
HST10
, Remark 6.40]
)
.

Define maps of stacks

(108)

\displaystyle{\rm spt}^{+}_{M},{\rm spt}^{-}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

as follows. Given an
S
-point
\phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
of the source, define objects
({\rm spt}^{+},\phi),({\rm spt}^{-},\phi)
in the groupoid
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))(S)
by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}Y^{c}=S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\subset S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}=Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\to}}M,\\
Y\setminus Y^{c}\simeq S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}Y^{+}\coprod Y^{-}&({\rm spt}^{+},\phi)\\
Y^{-}\coprod Y^{+}&({\rm spt}^{-},\phi)\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where
Y^{c}\hookrightarrow Y
(the inclusion of the
core
) is determined by the standard inclusion
0\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
. As indicated in the second line, the
+
or
-
decoration on
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
reflects the choice of the partition of the collar. By pulling back families, the above is natural in
S
and hence leads to the claimed maps (
108
).

Remark 4.4
.

A pair of
S
-points of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
determine isomorphic objects under
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
if the the maps
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
agree on a half-open neighborhood of the core
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\hookrightarrow S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
; see Definition
B.15
. Hence, the map of stacks (
108
) factors through the quotient of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
that parameterizes half-open germs of superpaths in
M
.

The following gives a map from super Euclidean paths in
M
to bordisms.

Definition 4.5

(Compare
[
HST10
, Definition 6.41]
)
.

Define maps of stacks

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\sf I}^{+}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\end{array}

as follows. Given
\Phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
and
(t,\theta)\colon S\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
, consider

(117)

\displaystyle S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\xrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{t,\theta}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}=\Sigma\xrightarrow{\Phi}M,

using the notation from (
66
).
To promote (
117
) to an object in the groupoid
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))(S)
, we require a choice of source and target data. Take

\displaystyle(Y_{{\rm in}}^{c},Y_{{\rm out}}^{c})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\Big(({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1})\Big)&\implies(I_{t,\theta}^{+},\Phi),&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}(S)\\
\Big(({\rm spt}^{-},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{-},\phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1})\Big)&\implies(I_{t,\theta}^{-},\Phi)&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}(S)\end{array}\right.

i.e., the ordering in the coproduct in (
117
) lists the source followed by the target
1|1
-Euclidean pair. The resulting options and the restriction on
(t,\theta)
in (
4.5
) follow from requiring the existence of collars for the source and target. Any pair of choices of collar provide isomorphic functors (
4.5
); see Remark
4.8
.

Remark 4.6
.

In parallel to Remark
4.4
,
S
-points of
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
determine isomorphic objects in
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
under
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
if the associated maps
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
agree on a neighborhood of the core of the bordism, and so the family pulls back from the associated quotient.

Changing the source and target data give variations on (
4.5
).

Definition 4.7

(Compare
[
HST10
, Definition 6.41]
)
.

Define maps of stacks

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\sf L}^{+}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf L}^{-}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)),\\
{\sf R}^{+}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf R}^{-}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\end{array}

as follows. Again for
\Phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
and
(t,\theta)\colon S\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
consider (
117
), but choose source and target data

\displaystyle(Y_{{\rm in}}^{c},Y_{{\rm out}}^{c})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\Big(\big(({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{-},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})\big),\emptyset\Big)&\implies(L_{t,\theta}^{+},\Phi)&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}(S)\\
\Big(\big(({\rm spt}^{-},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})\big),\emptyset\Big)&\implies(L_{t,\theta}^{-},\Phi)&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}(S)\\
\Big(\emptyset,\big(({\rm spt}^{-},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})\big)\Big)&\implies(R_{t,\theta}^{+},\Phi)&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}(S)\\
\Big(\emptyset,\big(({\rm spt}^{+},\Phi),({\rm spt}^{-},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})\big)\Big)&\implies(R_{t,\theta}^{-},\Phi)&(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}(S)\end{array}\right.

where the restriction on
(t,\theta)
is necessary for the existence of collars for the source and target. Any pair of choices provide isomorphic functors (
4.7
); see Remark
4.8
.

Remark 4.8
.

The requirements on
(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}(S)
in (
4.5
) and (
4.7
) come from demanding the existence of the collars
W_{{\rm in}/{\rm out}}^{\pm}
with properties demanded by Definition
B.15
; any pair of choices of
W_{{\rm in}/{\rm out}}^{\pm}
lead to isomorphic objects in
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
by intersecting the choices of collar.
We emphasize that the families
(R_{t,\theta}^{\pm},\Phi)
in (
4.5
) do not have a limit in
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
as
(t,\theta)\to 0
; compare
[
HST10
, Theorem 6.23]
.

Lemma 4.9
.

The restriction of the spin flip on
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
or
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
along
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
or
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
is determined by the maps

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),\quad\phi\mapsto\phi\circ{\sf fl}^{-1}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),\quad(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(t,-\theta,\phi\circ{\sf fl}^{-1})

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),\quad(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(t,-\theta,\phi\circ{\sf fl}^{-1})

Proof.

This follows from Definition
3.8
of the spin flip.
∎

4.3.
Composition of bordisms

The source and target of the families of bordisms
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
are specified in their definitions. This source and target data can equivalently be phrased in terms of 2-commuting diagrams of stacks. For example, we have

(131)

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\times{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
({\rm proj}_{+},{\rm act}_{+})
{\rm spt}^{+}_{M}\times{\rm spt}^{+}_{M}
{\sf s}\times{\sf t}

where
{\rm proj}
and
{\rm act}
are the projection and action maps

\displaystyle{\rm proj},\ {\rm act}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

(132)

\displaystyle{\rm proj}(t,\theta,\Phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle{\rm act}(t,\theta,\Phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1}.

for
(t,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}(S)
and
\Phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M
, and
{\rm proj}_{+},{\rm act}_{+}
(respectively,
{\rm proj}_{-},{\rm act}_{-}
) are the restrictions of
{\rm proj},{\rm act}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
(respectively,
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
).
The other structure maps in
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
lead to similar 2-commuting diagrams.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
{\sf u}
be the unit functor in
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
. There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(133)

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\rm spt}^{+}_{M}
i_{0}\times{{\rm id}}_{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)}
{\sf u}
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}

where the left vertical arrow is inclusion along
0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
. Let
{\sf c}
be the composition functor. There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(134)

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf I}^{+}_{M}\circ p_{2}\times{\sf I}^{+}_{M}\circ p_{1}
{\sf m}
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
{\sf c}

where the top horizontal arrow is determined by the family
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
and the maps

\displaystyle p_{i}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),

(135)

\displaystyle p_{1}\big((s,\eta),(t,\theta),\Phi\big)=(t,\theta,\Phi),\qquad p_{2}\big((s,\eta),(t,\theta),\Phi\big)=(s,\eta,\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})

and
{\sf m}
is inherited from multiplication in
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
,

(136)

\displaystyle{\sf m}\big((s,\eta),(t,\theta),\Phi\big)=(s+t+\eta\theta,\eta+\theta,\Phi).

There are analogous diagrams to (
133
) and (
134
) for the family of bordisms classified by
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
.

Proof.

The commutativity of the diagram (
133
) is almost immediate; the only subtlety comes from the choice of collars in the construction of the family
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
. Indeed, rather than a strictly commutative diagram this choice leads to 2-commutativity data gotten from the isomorphism between objects of
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
from shrinking the collar in (
4.3
).

Turning to the diagram (
134
), the definition of the
S
-family
(I_{t,\theta}^{+},\Phi)
of bordisms implies that the pair (
135
) is indeed in the fibered product. The construction of 2-commutativity data comes from unpacking the definition of the functor
{\sf c}
, i.e., composition of bordisms. Consider the diagram

(137)

\displaystyle S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\xhookrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{t,\theta}\coprod i_{(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M.

By forgetting any of the three inclusions of
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
, one obtains three possible
S
-points of the source of
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
via the input datum (
117
): forgetting
i_{(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)}
gives the
S
-family of bordisms
(I^{+}_{t,\theta},\Phi)
, forgetting
i_{t,\theta}
gives the
S
-family of bordisms
(I^{+}_{(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)},\Phi)
, and forgetting
i_{0}
gives a family that is isomorphic to
(I^{+}_{s,\eta},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})
(where the isomorphism is specified by
T_{t,\theta}
). These three
S
-families of bordisms gotten from (
137
) coincide with the two projections out of the fibered product and the value of the functor
{\sf c}
. The only subtle aspect in commutativity of the diagram (
134
) is the choice of collars in the definitions of these families of bordisms, i.e., the choices in the construction of the functor
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
. However, since any pair of choices lead to isomorphic bordisms (by intersecting the collars), we obtain canonical 2-commutativity data in (
134
).

The arguments involving
{\rm spt}^{-}_{M}
and
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
are identical.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

In terms of
S
-families of bordisms, the top horizontal map in (
134
) is

\big((s,\eta),(t,\theta),\Phi\big)\mapsto\big((I^{+}_{s,\eta},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}),(I^{+}_{t,\theta},\Phi)\big).

Notation 4.12
.

Let
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{0}
denote the composition
{\sf u}\circ{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
,

{\sf I}^{\pm}_{0}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)).

For an
S
-point
\Phi
of the source above, let
(I_{0}^{\pm},\Phi)
denote the associated
S
-family of (identity)
1|1
-Euclidean bordisms.

The following generalizes the adjunction between super semigroups coming from the last relation in (
49
).

Proposition 4.13
.

There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(138)

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf L}^{+}_{M}\circ p_{2}\times({\sf I}^{+}_{M}\circ p_{1}\coprod{\sf I}^{-}_{M}\circ p_{3})
{\sf L}^{+}_{M}
{\sf c}

where the top vertical arrow is determined by the families
{\sf L}^{+}_{M}
,
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
and
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
and the maps

\displaystyle p_{i}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),\quad i=1,2

(139)

\displaystyle p_{1}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),(r,\rho),\Phi)=(t,\theta,\Phi),\quad p_{2}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),(r,\rho),\Phi)=(s,\eta,\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})

\displaystyle p_{3}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

(140)

\displaystyle p_{3}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),(r,\rho),\Phi)=(r,\rho,\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}\circ T^{-1}_{s,\eta})

and the left vertical arrow is determined by the multiplication in
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
,

((t,\theta),(s,\eta),(r,\rho),\Phi)\mapsto((r,\rho)^{-1}\cdot(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta),\Phi).

There is a similar 2-commutative diagram for
{\sf L}^{-}_{M}
.

Proof.

From the definitions of source and target data, the top map in (
138
) does indeed yield an object in the fibered product. The 2-commutativity data comes from unpacking the definition of the functor
{\sf c}
. Similar to before, consider the diagram

S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\xhookrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{t,\theta}\coprod i_{(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)}\coprod i_{(r,\rho)^{-1}\cdot(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M.

In this case, forgetting any pair of factors in the coproduct gives the input data to an
S
-family of bordisms. For example, forgetting the middle two factors corresponds to the
S
-family of bordisms
(L^{+}_{(r,\rho)^{-1}\cdot(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)},\Phi)
, which is the claimed composition. The bordisms in (
139
) can similarly be extracted by forgetting the appropriate pair of inclusions and applying an appropriate isometry determined by a translation of
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

In terms of
S
-families of bordisms, the top horizontal map in (
138
) is

((t,\theta),(s,\eta),(r,\rho),\Phi)\mapsto\Big((L^{+}_{s,\eta},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}),\big((I^{+}_{t,\theta},\Phi)\coprod(I^{-}_{r,\rho},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}\circ T^{-1}_{s,\eta})\big)\Big).

The following shows that the family of bordisms
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
factors as a composition involving
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
. The proof uses similar techniques as above.

Proposition 4.15
.

There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(141)

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

({\sf L}_{M}^{+}\circ p_{1}\coprod{\sf I}_{0}^{+}\circ q_{2})\times({\sf I}_{0}^{+}\circ q_{1}\coprod{\sf R}^{+}_{M}\circ p_{2})
{\sf m}
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
{\sf c}

where the top vertical arrow is determined by the families
{\sf L}^{+}_{M}
,
{\sf R}^{+}_{M}
,
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{0}
, and the maps

\displaystyle p_{i}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

(142)

\displaystyle p_{1}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)=(t,\theta,\Phi),\quad p_{2}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)=(s,\eta,\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}),

\displaystyle q_{i}\colon\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\quad q_{1}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)=\Phi,\quad q_{2}((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)=\Phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1}\circ T_{s,\eta}^{-1}

and
{\sf m}
is (
136
). There is a similar 2-commutative diagram factoring
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
as a composition.

Remark 4.16
.

In terms of
S
-families of bordisms, the top horizontal arrow in (
141
) is

((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)\mapsto\Big((L^{+}_{t,\theta},\Phi)\coprod(I^{+}_{0},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}\circ T^{-1}_{s,\eta})\Big),\Big((I_{0}^{+},\Phi)\coprod(R_{s,\eta}^{+},\Phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta})\Big).

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}\circ\sigma
denote the functor
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
post-composed with the isomorphism in
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
that exchanges the order of components of the target, i.e.,

{\sf t}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{t,\theta}\circ\sigma,\Phi)=({\rm spt}^{\mp},\Phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1})\coprod({\rm spt}^{\pm},\Phi).

There is a 2-commutative triangle

(143)

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\Rightarrow

{\sf L}^{+}_{M}\circ\sigma
{\sf L}^{-}_{M}

for the left vertical arrow

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),

(144)

\displaystyle(t,\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(-t,-\theta,\phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1}).

Proof.

Starting with the data that determines
(L_{t,\theta}^{+},\Phi)
,

\displaystyle S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\coprod S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\xhookrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{t,\theta}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M.

exchanging the factors of the coproduct and acting on

by

provides the data determining

.
∎

We recall the super Euclidean loop space

from Definition
3.26
. By

, Lemma 2.12]
, there is a functor

whose source and target are the empty family of

-Euclidean manifolds.

Proposition 4.18
.

There is 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M))^{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\Phi\circ T_{t,\theta}^{-1}=\Phi\circ T_{s,\eta}^{-1}\iff\Phi=\Phi\circ(T_{(s,\eta)\cdot(t,\theta)^{-1}})^{n},\quad n\in{\mathbb{Z}}

the top vertical arrow is determined by the families

Proof.

The compatibility

with circumference

∎

Remark 4.19
.

In terms of
S

((t,\theta),(s,\eta),\Phi)\mapsto\Big((L^{-}_{t,\theta},\Phi),(R^{+}_{s,\eta},\Phi)\Big).

4.5.
Small bordisms and nearly constant superpaths

Definition 4.23
.

Consider the families of bordisms associated to the maps of stacks

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\

{\rm l}^{-}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf r}^{+}_{M}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\

by restriction of (

In particular, the families (

\displaystyle{\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\to{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)),\quad{\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)).

.

The data of an internal functor is reviewed in Definition

. Since the 2-isomorphism data in this proposition is canonical (coming from intersecting collars of bordisms), the coherence property demanded by these 2-isomorphisms is automatic. This completes the construction of the internal functor (

).

The compatibility of this functor with the additional structures follows from Propositions

and

∎

Remark 4.25
.

Composition and disjoint union of small bordisms generates a subcategory
1|1\hbox{-{\sf ebord}}(M)\subset 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
of small bordisms in
M
. Morally, the categories
1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
in Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
considers degree
n
field theories defined on these subcategories. However, it is difficult to formulate a precise notion of generators for a (sub)category in the framework from
[
ST11
]
, and so instead we formulate our results in terms of the further subcategory
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)\subset 1|1\hbox{-{\sf ebord}}(M)
.

5.
1|1
-Euclidean field theories of degree
n

In this section we review Stolz and Teichner’s degree
n
twist and degree
n
twisted field theories over
M
from
[
ST11
]
. In brief, these are internal natural transformations (
31
)
for a
\otimes
-invertible functor
\mathscr{T}
called the
degree twist
.
A complete construction of
\mathscr{T}
has yet to appear, but expected properties of
\mathscr{T}
are described in
[
ST04
,
HST10
,
ST11
]
for three variations on the definition of the bordism category
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
and Morita bicategory
{\sf{Alg}}
. With all of these slightly differing past formalisms and others under current development, our goal below is provide a conceptual description of the degree
n
twist that can be parsed in any framework for field theories. Our description relies on the cobordism hypothesis for 2-dimensional extended topological field theories. Assuming this hypothesis, one can calculate the values of the degree
n
twist and twisted field theories for the families of bordisms constructed in the previous section.
The punchline is that degree
n
field theories are (non-topological) boundary conditions for the 2-dimensional topological field theory determined by the Morita invertible (and hence fully-dualizable) algebra
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
, the
n
th Clifford algebra. The Morita equivalences between
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{m}
and
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n+m}
then give the desired (graded) multiplication on degree
n
field theories via the tensor product.

Remark 5.1
.

The values of the degree
n
twist for a
d
-dimensional Euclidean field theories without supersymmetry are listed in
[
ST11
, §5.3]
.
One possible way to extend this construction to (supersymmetric)
1|1
-Euclidean bordisms involves a generators and relations presentation for the bordism category. Such presentations have been described in
[
HST10
, 6.7]
,
[
ST11
, 3.2]
, and the proof of
[
ST11
, Proposition 4.16]
. However, complete statements and proofs have yet to appear.
In terms of these expected generators and relations,
1|1
-Euclidean field theories of degree
n
are equivalent to a quantum mechanical theory with the
n
th Clifford algebra
{\rm Cl}_{n}
acting by symmetries in the sense of §
2.4
, see
[
ST04
, §3.2]
and
[
HST10
, Main Theorem]
. The construction of the degree
n
twist outlined below is compatible with these prior expectations.

5.1.
The values of twisted field theories

Twisted geometric field theories are defined as internal natural transformations (
258
) for an internal functor
\mathscr{T}
from a geometric bordism category to the internal Morita category; see §
B.4
for a review. We unpack the values of a twisted
1|1
-Euclidean field theory on the families of bordisms from the previous section; this expands upon the discussion
[
ST11
, page 52]
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Given an internal functor
\mathscr{T}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
, a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted field theory
E
determines data

(1)

bundles of left modules

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}_{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),\quad_{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)\end{array}

where
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
are super algebra bundles over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
;

(2)

maps of bimodule bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle E({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\to{\rm act}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M}),

\displaystyle E({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle E({\sf R}^{+}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{+}_{M})\to{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M}),

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
,

\displaystyle E({\sf I}^{-}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{-}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{-}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\to{\sf t}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M}),

\displaystyle E({\sf L}^{-}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{-}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{-}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},

and maps of bimodule bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
,

\displaystyle E({\sf R}^{-}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{-}_{M})\to{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{-}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{-}_{M}),

for the projection and action maps

and

defined immediately after (

(3)

a section of the dual of the equivariant vector bundle

over the stack

that is invariant under the action of super Euclidean isometries on

.

Proof.

By Definition
B.7
of an internal functor, the twist determines maps of stacks

of super algebras;

•

-

-bimodules;

\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{-}_{M})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

-

-bimodules;

of right

-modules;

•

\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}_{M})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
of right

-modules;

•

\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{+}_{M})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
of left

-modules;

•

and
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M))\to\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
, a vector bundle.

\mu

147
). There is an isomorphism of right
{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-}_{M})
-module bundles

(160)

\displaystyle{\rm inv}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}_{M})\simeq\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\quad{\rm over}\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

where
{\rm inv}
is the map (
144
). The isomorphisms above associated to composition are subject to further coherence conditions, as dictated by the definition of an internal functor.

A twisted field theory is defined as an internal natural transformation (see Definition
B.8
), and hence is the data of a map of symmetric monoidal stacks with involution
\eta\colon{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}})
and compatibility data (
248
). The map of stacks
\eta
hence determines morphisms over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\in{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}}),\quad{\rm with}\quad{\sf t}(E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}))=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}),\ {\sf s}(E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}))=\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}.\end{array}

By the definition of
{\sf{Alg}}
, this provides the bundles of left modules in (1): the right action by
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
is no additional data. The data
\rho
of a twisted field theory yields the data in (2) and (3) by the argument from
[
ST11
, page 52]
. For example,
\rho
evaluated on the family
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
is a map of bimodules

E({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+}_{M})}{\rm proj}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})\to{\rm act}_{+}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{+}_{M})

which is the first map of bimodules listed above.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

The data in Proposition
5.2
satisfy compatibility properties with respect to composition of bordisms.
In particular, we have an equality of bimodule maps

(162)

\displaystyle p_{1}^{*}E({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\circ p_{2}^{*}E({\sf I}^{+}_{M})={\sf m}^{*}E({\sf I}^{+}_{M}),\quad{\rm over}\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

where
p_{1},p_{2}
are the maps (
135
). There is further an equality of bimodule maps

(163)

p_{2}^{*}E({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\circ(p_{1}^{*}E({\sf I}^{+}_{M})\otimes p_{3}^{*}E({\sf I}^{-}_{M}))={\sf m}^{*}E({\sf L}^{+}_{M}),\ {\rm over}\ {\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

where
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
are the maps in (
139
). We have the equality of dual sections of
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M))

(164)

\displaystyle p_{1}^{*}E({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\circ p_{2}^{*}E({\sf R}^{-}_{M})={\sf m}^{*}E(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)),\quad{\rm over}\quad\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)

for
p_{1},p_{2}
the projections to the factors in (
147
). Proposition
4.17
yields the equality of maps

(165)

\displaystyle{\rm inv}^{*}E({\sf L}^{-}_{M})=E({\sf L}^{+}_{M})\quad{\rm over}\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

where
{\rm inv}
is the map (
144
). Analogous statements hold after exchanging the
+
and
-
decorations for the families of bordisms.

Proof.

The claim follows from Definition
B.8
of an internal natural transformation, where the data in Proposition
5.2
must satisfy compatibility properties for composition in the internal category
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
and invariance properties for isomorphisms in the stack
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
. We emphasize that the relations demanded above are now
conditions
that certain maps of vector bundles be equal rather than additional data.

In more detail, (
162
) follows from compatibility with composition for the families
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
as computed in Proposition
4.10
. The equality (
163
) follows from compatibility for composition of the families
{\sf L}^{+}_{M}
and
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}\coprod{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
computed in Proposition
4.13
. The equality (
164
) follows from compatibility with the composition of
{\sf L}^{+}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{-}_{M}
computed in Proposition
4.18
. Finally, (
165
) follows from the isomorphism of families of bordisms computed in Proposition
4.17
.
∎

5.2.
Reflection and real structures

Next we describe reflection and real structures for twists and twisted field theories. We require a little notation: extend the conjugation functor on supermanifolds to a conjugation functor on generalized supermanifolds that sends a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
to the presheaf

\overline{\mathcal{F}}(S):=\mathcal{F}(\overline{S}).

We will phrase reflection positivity and reality data using the map of sheaves

(166)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)},

\displaystyle(\phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\overline{S}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{\overline{S}}\times{\sf r}}\overline{S}\times\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}\overline{M}\simeq M),

which also determines maps

(167)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times\overline{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)},

(168)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times\overline{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)}.

In an abuse of notation, the maps (
166
), (
167
) and (
168
) will all be denoted by
{\sf r}
. Proposition
4.20
gives 2-commuting data for the diagrams in stacks

{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
{\sf r}

\Rightarrow

{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
{\sf r}
\overline{{\sf I}}^{+}_{M}
{\sf R}_{1}

\Rightarrow

with similar diagrams for
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
. Next define the map

(169)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),

\displaystyle(\phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to M)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{\overline{S}}\times{\sf or}^{-1}}S\times\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{1|1}\to M),

which also determines maps

(170)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

The maps (

), (

) and (

) will all be denoted by
{\sf or}
.
Proposition
4.21
gives 2-commuting data

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}

{\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M}
{\sf Or}_{0}

\Rightarrow

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

{\sf I}^{+}_{M}
{\sf or}
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
{\sf Or}_{1}

with similar diagrams for
{\sf I}^{-}_{M}
,

and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
.

We recall from Definition
B.29
that reflection structures for twists and twisted field theories are defined in terms of
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariance data in the sense of Definition
B.13
for the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2

.

Lemma 5.4
.

A reflection structure for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
determines an invertible
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
-
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M}))}
-bimodule
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

and isomorphisms of bimodules (where the bimodule on the right is the identity bimodule)

(172)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\otimes{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{R}}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\mp}\simeq\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})

and isomorphisms of bimodules

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\otimes_{{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}}{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{\mp}_{M})},

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}({\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\mp})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}_{M})},

over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
, and

(173)

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{+}_{M})\xrightarrow{\sim}({\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\mp}\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm})\otimes_{{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}(\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})})}{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{-}_{M})},

over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
satisfying an involutive property, where above we have used the notation for values of the twist listed in the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Proof.

Equivariance data demands an internal natural isomorphism, which (from Definition
B.8
) is a map of stacks

\phi\colon{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt}))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}})^{\times}

valued in invertible bimodules, together with coherence data
\rho
satisfying properties. Applying
\phi
to the family
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
, we obtain the claimed bundle of invertible bimodules. Applying
\rho
to the families
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
from §
4.2
, a reflection structure specifies data of isomorphisms of bimodules involving the values of the twist listed in the proof of Proposition
5.2
. The other
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariance datum is an isomorphism
\beta
between internal natural isomorphisms, see (
250
); this determines the isomorphism of bimodules (
172
).
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Fix a reflection structure for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
. Then a reflection structure for a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted field theory is an isomorphism of bundles of left
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
-modules over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

(174)

\displaystyle E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\otimes_{{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}}{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M}))}.

These isomorphisms are required to satisfy compatibility conditions for the families
{\sf L}^{\pm}(M)
,
{\sf R}^{\pm}(M)
, and
{\sf I}^{\pm}(M)
, namely commutative squares,

(175)

\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\Big(\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes_{\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}}\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}\Big)
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}
E({\sf I}^{\pm}_{M})
\simeq
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{E({\sf I}^{\mp}_{M})}
\simeq

(176)

\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes_{\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}}\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\underline{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}}}
E({\sf L}^{+}_{M})
\simeq
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{E({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})}
\simeq

over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
, and commutative squares over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{<0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)

\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}{\rm proj}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})\otimes{\rm act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\underline{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}}}
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes_{\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}}\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\mp}_{M})}
E({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})
\simeq
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{E({\sf R}^{\mp}_{M})}
\simeq

where the vertical arrows use data from Lemma
5.4
and (
174
), though we have suppressed the tensor products with the bimodules
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}
that mediate between
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm})
-modules and
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\mp})}
-modules to avoid (further) cluttering the notation.

Proof.

Reflection positivity data for a twisted field theory is defined as an isomorphism between internal natural transformations, see Definition
B.14
. From Definition
B.8
, this is an isomorphism between the maps of stacks
{\rm Ob}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}})
that are part of the data of the internal natural transformation. Evaluating this isomorphism between maps of stacks on the families
{\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}
yields the data (
174
).

For the data (
174
) to determine an isomorphism between internal natural transformations, there are compatibility requirements for each object of
{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
. Evaluating on the families
{\sf I}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
recovers the claimed commutative squares.
∎

We may consider real structures for twists and twisted field theories in the sense of Definition
B.32
, using the real structure on
1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
from Lemma
4.2
, inherited from the real structure on the
1|1
-Euclidean geometry determined by (
71
).

Lemma 5.6
.

A real structure for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
determines an invertible
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
-
{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}))}
-bimodule
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M)
with involutive data analogous to (

) and isomorphisms of bimodules analogous to those in Lemma
5.4
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
5.4
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Fix a real structure for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
. Then a real structure for a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted field theory is an isomorphism of bundles of left
\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})
-modules

(177)

\displaystyle E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{T}}^{\pm}\otimes_{{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})}}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M}))}

over

. These isomorphisms are required to satisfy compatibility properties analogous to those in Lemma

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma

.
∎

5.3.
Invertible twists

The twists of interest for the conjectures (
1
) are the degree
n
twists for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. These are determined by an invertible twist

(178)

\displaystyle d|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})\xrightarrow{\mathscr{T}}{\sf{Alg}}^{\times},\qquad d=1,2

which then gives the degree
n
twists
\mathscr{T}^{\otimes n}\colon d|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}^{\times}
by taking tensor powers and precomposing with the canonical functor
d|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to d|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})
determined by the map
p\colon M\to{\rm pt}
. The twists
\mathscr{T}^{\otimes n}
give a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading on the associated collection of degree
n
twisted field theories for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. This grading is compatible with the tensor product of field theories: the tensor product of a degree
n
and degree
m
field theory is a field theory of degree
n+m
. Under the conjectured maps (
1
), the tensor product is expected to be compatible with the product in cohomology.

When
d=1
, (
178
) determines an invertible object of the (usual) Morita bicategory,

(179)

\displaystyle A:=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+})

gotten from evaluatation on the family of bordisms
{\rm spt}^{+}
over
{\rm pt}={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},{\rm pt})
from Definition
4.3
, using that an algebra bundle over
{\rm pt}
is just an algebra. By the cobordism hypothesis,
A
determines a 2-dimensional topological field theory. Such a topological theory also determines an invertible twist via the composition

(180)

\displaystyle 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})\to 1\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}({\rm pt})\hookrightarrow 2\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}({\rm pt})\xrightarrow{{\sf TFT}(A)}{\sf{Alg}}

where the first arrow is a forgetful functor from
1|1
-Euclidean bordisms to 1-dimensional spin bordisms, and the second arrow includes 1-dimensional bordisms into the (fully extended, spin) 2-dimensional bordism category. The arrow labeled
{\sf TFT}(A)
takes the fully-extended spin topological field theory determined by the invertible (and hence dualizable) algebra
A
.

Hypothesis 5.8
.

When
d=1
, an invertible twist
\mathscr{T}
is equivalent to the invertible twist (
180
) constructed from the value (
179
) of
\mathscr{T}
on
{\rm spt}^{+}
.

Assuming this hypothesis, we can compute the values of the twist on various families of bordisms over
M={\rm pt}
(compare Proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle A=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+}),\quad A^{\sf{op}}=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-}),\quad_{A}A_{A}=\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+}),\quad_{A^{\sf{op}}}{A^{\sf{op}}}_{A^{\sf{op}}}=\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{-}),

(181)

\displaystyle A/[A,A]=\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}({\rm pt}))),\quad_{\mathbb{C}}A_{A\otimes A^{\sf{op}}}=\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{+}),\quad_{\mathbb{C}}A_{A^{\sf{op}}\otimes A}=\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}),

\ {}_{A^{\sf{op}}\otimes A}A_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{+}),\quad_{A\otimes A^{\sf{op}}}A_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{-}),

where the above are interpreted as bimodule bundles over the corresponding family parameter for the bordisms involved. For example,
\mathscr{T}({\sf I}^{+})
is the trivial bundle over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
with fiber
A
as a bimodule over itself. The twist also requires the data of isomorphisms between various compositions of the above bordisms; this data is canonical in the case above, e.g., concatenation of superpaths corresponds to the isomorphisms

A\otimes_{A}A\simeq A,\qquad A^{\sf{op}}\otimes_{A^{\sf{op}}}A^{\sf{op}}\simeq A^{\sf{op}},

and the relation amongst bordisms in Proposition
4.18
corresponds to the canonical isomorphism

A\otimes_{A\otimes A^{\sf{op}}}A\simeq A/[A,A].

We refer to Definitions
B.30
and
B.32
for reflection and real data for a twist. Again by the cobordism hypothesis, reflection and real data for the twist determined by (
179
) amounts to additional structure on
A
. By Lemma
5.4
, a reflection structure for the degree twist can be specified by an anti-linear anti-involution

A=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{-})}=\overline{A}{}^{\sf{op}},

which allows us to view
A
itself as a
A
-
\overline{A}^{\sf{op}}
bimodule.
This is the data of a
*
-superalgebra structure, which is equivalent to the data of an ordinary
*
-structure on
A
; see §
A.2
. Similarly, Lemma
5.6
shows that a real structure for the twist is determined by an anti-linear involution

A=\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\rm spt}^{+})}=\overline{A}.

i.e., a real structure on
A
.

5.4.
The degree twist and degree
n
field theories

For
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded algebras over
{\mathbb{C}}
, any
\otimes
-invertible algebra is Morita equivalent to
{\mathbb{C}}
or
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
. Hence, assuming Hypothesis
5.8
, the degree twist is necessarily equivalent to the twist determined by
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
.

Hypothesis 5.9
.

The degree twist
\mathscr{T}=\mathscr{T}^{\otimes 1}
is determined under (
179
) by the Clifford algebra,
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
. A reflection structure for the degree twist is fixed by the standard
*
-structure (
233
) on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
. A real structure for the twist is fixed by the standard real structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the degree
n
twist is determined by the
n
th Clifford algebra
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
, since
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-1}
is the
\otimes
-inverse to
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}^{\otimes n}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
for
n\geq 0
. The real and reflection structure on the degree twists uniquely determines a real and reflection structure on the degree
n
twist.

Remark 5.10
.

The 2-dimensional spin topological field theory associated to a Clifford algebra has been studied in detail by Gunningham
[
Gun16
]
.

Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
extract the following data from a degree
n
theory.

Corollary 5.11
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, a degree
n
twisted field theory
E
determines

•

bundles of left
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-modules

{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}:=E({\rm spt}^{\pm}_{M})\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M);

•

maps of bundles of

over
