.

We observe that the data and structures in Corollaries
5.11
,
5.12
, and
5.13
can be restricted along the inclusions

(187)

\displaystyle M\subset\Pi TM\simeq{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),

yielding bundles of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-bundles

V_{\pm}:=\mathcal{V}_{\pm}|_{M},\qquad\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})\simeq\mathcal{V}_{\pm}|_{\Pi TM}.

where the isomorphism follows from Lemma
A.20
. In particular, the restriction of (
182
) and (
183
) combine to give a map

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}\Big(\overline{V}_{\pm}\otimes V_{\pm}\Big)\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}\Big(V_{\mp}\otimes V_{\pm}\Big)\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

which determines a pairing for which the Clifford actions are adjoint,

(188)

\displaystyle\langle-,-\rangle\colon\overline{V}_{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}V_{+}\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},\qquad\langle\omega^{*}\cdot x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|\omega||x|}\langle x,\omega\cdot y\rangle,\ \omega\in{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}.

Lemma 5.14
.

The pairing (
188
) is hermitian in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded sense

(189)

\displaystyle\langle x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|x||y|}\overline{\langle y,x\rangle}.

Proof.

The argument uses similar ideas to the first part of
[
HST10
, Corollary 6.25]
, though the signs here are different as we are working in the oriented setting (versus the unoriented framework of
[
HST10
]
).
Equation (
189
) follows from commutativity of the diagram

(190)

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}({\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\pm}\otimes{\rm act}_{\mp}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm})
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}({\rm proj}_{\mp}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{\mp}\otimes{\rm act}_{\mp}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm})
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\Big(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}}(\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\pm}\otimes\overline{{\rm act}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mp})\big)
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}}
{\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\big(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}}(\overline{{\rm act}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mp}\otimes\overline{{\rm proj}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\pm})
E({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})
\overline{E({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})}
\simeq
\sigma
\overline{{\rm inv}}^{*}\overline{E({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})}
restricts to
\langle-,-\rangle

where the
*
-structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
is used implicitly throughout. As indicated, the upper right arrow restricts to the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
by definition.
Commutativity of the lower triangle follows from (
165
). Commutativity of the inner square is the condition (
185
) on reflection positivity data.
The composition of arrows starting from the top left and using the lower triangle is the right hand side of (
189
), and hence the restriction of this diagram along (
187
) implies the claimed formula.
∎

Remark 5.15
.

We comment in more detail about the differing signs in the above argument and
[
HST10
, Corollary 6.25]
.
First, in the unoriented framework of
[
HST10
]
there is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear isomorphism
{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}\simeq{\sf or}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{\mp}
. This results in a
{\mathbb{C}}
-bilinear pairing through a definition analogous to (
188
). Second,
{\sf or}^{2}=(-1)^{\sf F}
whereas
\overline{({\sf r}\circ{\sf or})}\circ({\sf r}\circ{\sf or})={{\rm id}}
. This extra factor of
(-1)^{\sf F}
results in a pairing that is naively symmetric in
[
HST10
, Corollary 6.25]
, as compared with the graded symmetry of (
189
).

We refer to (
223
) for a discussion of the signs involved when formulating the positivity property for a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded hermitian pairing.

Definition 5.16
.

A
1|1
-Euclidean field theory of degree
n
with reflection positivity data is
reflection positive
if the pairing (
188
) is a (graded) positive-definite hermitian pairing on the super vector bundle
V_{+}
over
M
.

Remark 5.17
.

For a reflection positive field theory, (
188
) implies that the
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-action on
V_{+}
is automatically a
*
-representation relative to the hermitian inner product.

Remark 5.18
.

We contrast Definition
5.16
with the definition of positivity in
[
HST10
]
, where the
1|1
-Euclidean bordism category includes the orientation reversing map as an isometry. By functoriality, this orientation-reversing isometry must be sent to a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map. In particular, the positivity condition in
[
HST10
, Definition 6.26 and 6.49]
is not equivalent to reflection positivity above, where orientation-reversal corresponds to a
{\mathbb{C}}
-antilinear map. However, after one imposes real structures on field theories, positivity in the sense of
[
HST10
]
implies the real reflection-positive structure in the sense above.

6.
Extracting geometric data from a representations of superpaths

In this section we consider the restrictions (
32
) of degree
n
field theories along the functor
\iota
constructed in §
4
. From this we extract Clifford linear superconnections with additional structure and property.

6.1.
Twisted representations of super Lie categories

We begin with some general definitions of representations of super Lie categories. The strict 2-category of super Lie categories, smooth functors, and smooth natural transformations is recalled in Definition
B.9
. Throughout, a vector bundle is taken to be a locally free sheaf of nuclear Fréchet spaces; we make no finite rank assumption. The following generalizes the standard definition of a representation of a Lie groupoid, e.g., see
[
Mac87
, Definition 1.7.1]
.

Definition 6.1
.

A
representation
of a super Lie category
{\sf C}
is the data of a vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}\to{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
and a map of vector bundles
\rho\colon{\sf s}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}\to{\sf t}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}
over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
. This data is required to satisfy the condition
{\sf u}^{*}\rho={{\rm id}}_{\mathcal{V}}
on
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
and
p_{1}^{*}\rho\circ p_{2}^{*}\rho={\sf c}^{*}\rho
on
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})\times_{{\rm Ob}({\sf C})}{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
. An
isomorphism

({\mathcal{V}},\rho)\simeq({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
between representations is an isomorphism of vector bundles
{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
for which
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
satisfy a compatibility property over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

Example 6.2
.

A supermanifold
S
determines a super Lie category
{\sf C}
with
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})={\rm Mor}({\sf C})=S
, i.e., a discrete super Lie category. In this case, a representation of
{\sf C}
is the same data as a vector bundle over
S
.

Example 6.3
.

Let
{\sf C}
be the super Lie category with a single object and
{\rm Mor}({\sf C)}={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
, where composition is inherited from the super semigroup structure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
. Then a representation of
{\sf C}
as a super Lie category is equivalent to a super semigroup representation (
45
).

Definition 6.4
.

A
twist

\mathscr{T}
for a super Lie category
{\sf C}
is the data of:

(1)

\mathscr{T}_{0}\to{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
a bundle of algebras;

(2)

\mathscr{T}_{1}\to{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
a bundle of invertible
{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}
-
{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}
-bimodules;

(3)

an isomorphism of bimodule bundles
\epsilon\colon{\sf u}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathscr{T}_{0}
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
where
\mathscr{T}_{0}
is regarded as a bimodule over itself;

(4)

a map of bimodule bundles
\mu\colon p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{p_{2}^{*}{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\sf c}^{*}B\mathscr{T}_{1}
over the fibered product of pairs of composable morphisms,
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})\times_{{\rm Ob}({\sf C})}{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

The maps of
\mathscr{T}_{0}
-bimodules are required to satisfy further coherence conditions for composition with the identity and associativity of composition (see Lemma
6.10
). An
isomorphism

\mathscr{T}\simeq\mathscr{T}^{\prime}
between twists is an invertible
\mathscr{T}_{0}
-
\mathscr{T}_{0}^{\prime}
bimodule
\mathscr{B}
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
and an isomorphism
\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{B}\simeq{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{B}\otimes_{{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}^{\prime}}\mathscr{T}_{1}^{\prime}
of bimodules over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
satisfying a property over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})\times_{{\rm Ob}({\sf C})}{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

Remark 6.5
.

When
{\sf C}
is a Lie groupoid and the fibers of
\mathscr{T}_{0}\to{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
are central simple algebras, the above definition reduces to Freed’s notion of an invertible algebra bundle over
{\sf C}

[
Fre12
, Definition 1.59]
. In particular, gerbes provide examples of twists for the (Čech) groupoid associated to an open cover
[
Fre12
, Example 1.74]
. Invertible algebra bundles over quotient Lie groupoids determine twists for (equivariant) K-theory
[
Fre12
, Definition 1.78]
, see also Karoubi
[
Kar68
]
and Donovan–Karoubi
[
DK70
]
. All the examples of twistings below will have algebra bundles whose fibers are central simple.

Remark 6.6
.

Definition
6.4
is set up so that Lemma
6.10
holds using
[
ST11
, Definition 5.1]
for the internal Morita category
{\sf{Alg}}
. As a consequence, Definition
6.4
does not play well with equivalences of super Lie categories, see Remark
B.11
. There are various well-known ways to fix this issue.
For example, define a new version of twisting consisting of an equivalence of super Lie categories
{\sf C}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf C}
and a twist for
{\sf C}^{\prime}
in the above sense; this is essentially the definition of twistings for the K-theory of topological groupoids in
[
FHT11
]
. In all the examples below, twistings fit into the more naive framework of Definition
6.4
.

Definition 6.7
.

Given a twist for a super Lie category
{\sf C}
, a
twisted representation is

(1)

\eta\colon{\mathcal{V}}\to{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
a bundle of left
\mathscr{T}_{0}
-modules;

(2)

\rho\colon\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\sf s}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}\to{\sf t}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}
a map of bundles of left
{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}
-modules over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})

with the condition that the module maps (2) are compatible with composition in
{\sf C}
. Given an isomorphism between twists, and isomorphism between twisted representations
({\mathcal{V}},\rho)\simeq({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of left modules
\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}\simeq{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
with a compatibility condition over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

Definition 6.8
.

For any super Lie category
{\sf C}
and super algebra
A
, define the twist
\mathscr{T}_{A}
given by the trivial bundle of algebras over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
with fiber
A
, and trivial bundle of bimodules over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
with fiber
A
regarded as a bimodule over itself. The isomorphism
\epsilon
is the identity, and
\mu
is determined by the isomorphism
A\otimes_{A}A\simeq A
. We observe that Morita equivalent algebras give rise to isomorphic twists. For
A={\mathbb{C}}
, the twist
\mathscr{T}_{\mathbb{C}}
is the
trivial twist
, which we will also denote by
\mathbbold{1}\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf{Alg}}
.

The following is immediate from the definitions, compare
[
ST11
, Lemma 5.7]
.

Lemma 6.9
.

A twisted representation for the trivial twist determines a representation of
{\sf C}
in the sense of Definition
6.1
.

Lemma 6.10
.

A twist for a super Lie category
{\sf C}
is the same as an internal functor
\mathscr{T}\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf{Alg}}
, and a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted representation is equivalent to an internal natural transformation

{\sf C}
{\sf{Alg}},
E\Downarrow
\mathbbold{1}
\mathscr{T}

where
{\sf{Alg}}
is the internal Morita category (see Definition
B.20
). Isomorphisms between twists in the sense of Definition
6.4
are equivalent to internal natural isomorphisms between
\mathscr{T},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf{Alg}}
. Isomorphisms between twisted representations in the sense of Definition
6.7
are the same as isomorphisms between internal natural transformations
E\simeq E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The claim follows from unpacking the definitions. Super Lie categories can be regarded as categories internal to stacks; see §
B.3
below. Using that
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
and
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
are representable stacks, the data of the internal functor
\mathscr{T}
(see Definition
B.7
) is the same as a twist in the sense of Definition
6.4
. The data of an internal natural transformation
E
(see Definition
B.8
) is equivalent to a twisted representation
({\mathcal{V}},\rho)
in the sense of Definition
6.7
. The statements for isomorphisms between twists and isomorphisms between twisted representations follow similarly.
∎

Example 6.11
.

Let
{\sf C}
be the super Lie category from Example
6.3
. Consider the twist from Definition
6.8
relative to the algebra
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
.
Then a twisted representation of
{\sf C}
is a Clifford linear super semigroup representation
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\to{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}})
for a
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-module
{\mathcal{V}}
.

6.2.
Real representations

Lemma
6.10
allows us to import Stolz and Teichner’s notions of Real representations (e.g.,
[
HST10
, §6.8]
) to the setting of super Lie categories. This turns out to provide a generalization of Atiyah’s Real vector bundles, see Example
6.16
below. To start, we observe that the conjugation functor on supermanifolds (see Definition
A.18
) induces a strict involution on the strict 2-category of super Lie categories. We use the following notation and terminology for the values of this involution.

Definition 6.12
.

For a super Lie category
{\sf C}
, the
conjugate
super Lie category, denoted
\overline{\sf C}
, is defined by applying the conjugation functor on supermanifolds to the objects, morphisms, and structure maps of
{\sf C}
. Similarly, for a smooth functor
F\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf D}
between super Lie categories, let
\overline{F}\colon\overline{\sf C}\to\overline{\sf D}
be the
conjugate
smooth functor between conjugate categories, and for a smooth natural transformation
\eta\colon F\Rightarrow G
, let
\overline{\eta}\colon\overline{F}\Rightarrow\overline{G}
denote the
conjugate
smooth natural transformation between the conjugate smooth functors.

The following is a special case of a real structure on a category internal to stacks, see Definition
B.12
.

Definition 6.13
.

A
Real
structure on a super Lie category
{\sf C}
is a smooth functor
{\mathscr{R}}\colon{\sf C}\to\overline{\sf C}
and a smooth natural isomorphism
\eta\colon{{\rm id}}_{\sf C}\Rightarrow\overline{{\mathscr{R}}}\circ{\mathscr{R}}
. A super Lie category with a real structure is a
Real super Lie category
, denoted
({\sf C},{\mathscr{R}},\eta)
.

The data of the natural isomorphism
\eta\colon{{\rm id}}_{\sf C}\Rightarrow\overline{{\mathscr{R}}}\circ{\mathscr{R}}
will often be trivial in examples below, and so we often omit it from the notation. Real (twisted) representations of a super Lie category are then defined in terms of
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariance data for the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action on
{\sf C}
given by a Real structure, and the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action on
{\sf{Alg}}
given by complex conjugation of vector bundles.
The following is a special case of Definitions
B.13
and
B.14
.

Definition 6.14
.

For
{\sf C}
be a super Lie category with Real structure
{\mathscr{R}}
, a
Real structure
for a twist
\mathscr{T}
is an internal natural isomorphism
\phi_{\mathscr{T}}

{\sf C}
\overline{\sf C}
{\sf{Alg}}
{\sf{Alg}},
\phi_{\mathscr{T}}\ \rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\Rightarrow$}
\mathscr{T}
{\mathscr{R}}
\overline{\mathscr{T}}
(\overline{\phantom{A}})

and an isomorphism
\beta_{\mathscr{T}}
of internal natural transformations
\phi_{\mathscr{T}}^{2}\circ\eta_{\sf C}\simeq\eta_{{\sf{Alg}}}={{\rm id}}
, see (
250
). The data
(\mathscr{T},\phi_{\mathscr{T}},\beta_{\mathscr{T}})
is a
Real twist
. A
Real structure
for a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted representation
E
is a Real structure for
\mathscr{T}
and an isomorphism between internal natural transformations
\phi_{\mathscr{T}}\circ E\simeq E\circ\phi{1}
compatible with
\beta_{\mathscr{T}}
using the canonical real structure for the internal functor
\mathbbold{1}
.

Lemma 6.15
.

For a Real super Lie category
({\sf C},{\mathscr{R}})
and twist
\mathscr{T}
, a Real structure on
\mathscr{T}
determines data

(i)

a bundle
N\to{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
of invertible
\mathscr{T}_{0}
-
{{\mathscr{R}}_{0}}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}
bimodules with an isomorphism

(191)

\displaystyle N\otimes_{\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\overline{{\mathscr{R}}}_{0}}^{*}\overline{N}\simeq\mathscr{T}_{0}

of bimodule bundles over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
;

(ii)

an isomorphism of
{\sf t}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}
-
{\sf s}^{*}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}
bimodule bundles over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})

\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\sf s}^{*}N\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf t}^{*}N\otimes_{{\sf t}^{*}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{1}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{1}}

satisfying a compatibility condition on
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})\times_{{\rm Ob}({\sf C})}{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

Given a Real twist, a Real twisted representation determines additional data

(iii)

an isomorphism of left
\mathscr{T}_{0}
-modules
N\otimes_{{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{V}}
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})

satisfying the property that the diagram of
{\sf t}^{*}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}
-
{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}
bimodule bundles over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
commutes

(192)

\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\sf s}^{*}(N\otimes_{{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}})
{\sf t}^{*}N\otimes_{{\sf t}^{*}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{1}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{T}_{1}}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\sf s}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}})
\mathscr{T}_{1}\otimes_{{\sf s}^{*}\mathscr{T}_{0}}{\sf s}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}
{\sf t}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}
{\sf t}^{*}(N\otimes_{{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}})
\rho
{\rm{\rm(ii)}}\otimes{{\rm id}}
{{\rm id}}\otimes{\rm(iii)}
{{\rm id}}\otimes{\mathscr{R}}_{1}^{*}\overline{\rho}
{\sf t}^{*}{\rm(iii)}

with maps defined by the data above as indicated.

Proof.

By Definition
B.8
, the first piece of data of the internal natural transformation
\phi_{\mathscr{T}}
is a map of stacks

\displaystyle N\colon{\rm Ob}({\sf C})\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}}),

which by the Yoneda lemma is equivalent to a bundle of bimodules over the supermanifold
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})
. The source of this bimodule is the algebra bundle
{\mathscr{R}}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}}_{0}
, and the target is
\mathscr{T}_{0}
. As the internal natural transformation is invertible, this bundle of bimodules is also required to be invertible. The isomorphism (
191
) is the datum
\beta_{\mathscr{T}}
. This provides the data in part (i) of the lemma. Part (ii) supplies the datum denoted
\rho
in (
248
) for an internal natural transformation. The condition on the data (i) and (ii) similarly follows from the definition of an internal natural transformation over
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
and
{\rm Mor}({\sf C})\times_{{\rm Ob}({\sf C})}{\rm Mor}({\sf C})
.

The data of an isomorphism between internal natural transformations is an isomorphism between their corresponding maps of stacks
{\rm Ob}({\sf C})\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{Alg}})
; see Definition
B.8
. In this case, this is precisely the datum (iii). The diagram (
192
) is the coherence condition so that this isomorphism of maps of stacks determines an isomorphism between internal natural transformations.
∎

Example 6.16
.

For an ordinary manifold
X
regarded as a super Lie category, an involution
\alpha\colon X\to X
determines a Real structure on
X
by setting
{\mathscr{R}}
to be the composition

X\xrightarrow{\alpha}X\simeq\overline{X}

for the canonical real structure on
X
coming from complex conjugation of smooth functions. Taking the trivial twist with its canonical Real structure, a Real representation of
(X,\alpha)
is then the same data as a Real vector bundle in the sense of Atiyah
[
Ati66
]
.

6.3.
Degree
n
representations of superpaths

Now we specialize to the super Lie category
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
of nearly constant superpaths in
M
.

Definition 6.17
.

The
degree
n
twist
for the super Lie category
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is the coproduct of trivial algebra bundles on objects,

\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{n}\coprod\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{-n}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)={\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))

and the coproduct of trivial bimodule bundles over morphisms

\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{n}\coprod\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{-n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)={\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M)).

The remaining data for this twist is specified by the identity isomorphism
\epsilon={{\rm id}}\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
and the canonical isomorphisms of Clifford bimodules,
\mu\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
(for
\epsilon
and
\mu
in the notation of Definition
6.4
). The required properties in this case are trivially satisfied.

Definition 6.18
.

A
degree
n
representation
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is a twisted representation for the degree
n
twist.

Proposition 6.19
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the restriction of a degree
n
field theory along (
32
) determines a degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
.

Proof.

The value of the restriction on objects and morphisms of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is immediate from Corollary
5.11
, after further restricting the data there to the subspace

(193)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1},M),

which includes along the
S
-families of maps that factor through the quotient

S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\to(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/{\mathbb{R}}\simeq S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\to M.

The compatibility with source, target, and units is clear; compatibility with composition comes from restricting (
162
) along (
193
).
∎

Proposition 6.20
.

A degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)

determines
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-linear superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
on super vector bundles
V_{\pm}\to M
.

Proof.

Following Definition
6.7
, let
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{+}\coprod{\mathcal{V}}_{-}
denote the value of the representation on objects, where
{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
is a bundle of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-modules and
{\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
. Using Lemma
A.20
, we make the identification

(194)

\displaystyle\Gamma({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M);{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm})\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm}),\qquad V_{\pm}={\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}|_{M}

for vector bundles
V_{\pm}\to M
gotten by restriction along the canonical inclusion
M\hookrightarrow{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
.

The map of left
A
-modules in Definition
6.7
translates in this case to maps
\rho_{\pm}
of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-module bundles

\displaystyle\rho_{+}\colon{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{+}\to{\rm act}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{+}\ {\rm over}\ {\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{1|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

\displaystyle\rho_{-}\colon{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\to{\rm act}_{-}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\ {\rm over}\ {\mathbb{R}}_{\leq 0}^{1|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

for the projection and action maps gotten by restriction of (
132
).
Using (
194
) and applying Lemma
3.18
,
\rho_{\pm}
are maps of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
-modules for the action on the source and target gotten by pulling back along the projection or the restriction of (
86
). This gives the description

(195)

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta,\alpha v)=(\alpha-\theta d\alpha)\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta,v),

\displaystyle\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M),\ v\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})

for the standard coordinates
t
and
\theta
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}
.
Compatibility with composition promotes
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{-}
to super semigroup representations, since multiplication in
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}
corresponds to composition in
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
.

Taylor expanding (
195
) in the odd variable
-\theta\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0})
, define

(196)

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta,-)=A_{\pm}(t)-\theta B_{\pm}(t)

where
A_{\pm}(t)\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})
is an even map and
B_{\pm}(t)\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})
is odd, and both commute with the Clifford actions. Define

(197)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}:=B_{\pm}(0)\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm})\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\pm}).

Since
B_{\pm}(t)
is odd, so is
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
. Furthermore, because
B_{\pm}(t)
is
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-linear, so is
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
. Finally, combining (
195
) and (
196
) yields the pair of equations

A_{\pm}(t)(\alpha v)=\alpha A_{\pm}(t)(v),\qquad B_{\pm}(t)(\alpha v)=d\alpha A_{\pm}(t)(v)+(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha B_{\pm}(t)(v).

Using that
A_{\pm}(0)={{\rm id}}_{V_{\pm}}
, the value of the second equation at
t=0
is the Leibniz rule for the superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
.
∎

Under the above translation, the renormalization group flow corresponds to a standard structure in index theory.

Definition 6.21

(
[
BGV92
, page 267]
)
.

Define the
(Getzler) rescaling
of a Clifford linear superconnection by the
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-action (
34
).

The action of the renormalization group on
1|1
-Euclidean bordisms is computed in Lemma
4.22
and restricts to the category of nearly constant superpaths via (
157
).

Proposition 6.22
.

The renormalization group flow on a degree
n
field theory restricts along (
32
) to determine the rescaling of the superconnections extracted in Proposition
6.20
.

Proof.

Using (
157
), we can compute using the action of the renormalization group on
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
. This action was computed in (
94
). In particular, we have an isomorphism

{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf RG}_{\mu}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm},\qquad s\mapsto\mu^{{\rm deg}/2}s,\qquad s\in\Gamma({\mathcal{V}}_{\pm})

viewing the source and target as
\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
-modules as in (
194
), where the target module structure is twisted by pulling back along the RG-action. Hence, under the above identification a representation
\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta)
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is sent under
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
to
\mu^{-{\rm deg}/2}\rho(\mu^{2}t,\mu\theta)\mu^{{\rm deg}/2}
. From the definition (
197
) of the superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{+}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{-}
in terms of the representations
\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta)
this yields the formula (
34
).
∎

6.4.
Degree
n
representations with a pairing

The restriction of the map (
144
) determines

{\rm inv}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M),\quad(t,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(-t,-\theta,\phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta}).

Lemma 6.23
.

The pullback of
\rho_{-}
along
{\rm inv}
gives a map of vector bundles

{\rm inv}^{*}\rho_{-}\colon{\rm act}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\to{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}

over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
commuting with the
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}
-action.

Proof.

We observe that the triangles commute,

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\rm inv}
{\rm act}_{+}
{\rm proj}_{-}

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
{\rm inv}
{\rm proj}_{+}
{\rm act}_{-}

using that

{\rm act}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\phi)=\phi\circ T^{-1}_{t,\theta},\qquad{\rm proj}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\phi)=\phi.

Hence, we have

{\rm act}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\simeq{\rm inv}^{*}{\rm proj}_{-}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\xrightarrow{{\rm inv}^{*}\rho_{-}}{\rm inv}^{*}{\rm act}_{-}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\simeq{\rm proj}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}

so
{\rm inv}^{*}\rho_{-}
uniquely determines a map with the claimed source and target.
∎

Definition 6.24
.

A
pairing
on a degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is a vector bundle map over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

(198)

\displaystyle L\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+})\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

with the property that the diagram of vector bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
commutes

(199)

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\rm act}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}{\mathcal{V}}_{+})
{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+}))
{\rm act}^{*}_{+}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+}))
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}.
{{\rm id}}\otimes\rho_{+}
{\rm inv}^{*}\rho_{-}\otimes{{\rm id}}
{\rm proj}_{+}^{*}L
{\rm act}_{+}^{*}L

Lemma 6.25
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the restriction of a degree
n
field theory along (
32
) equips the resulting degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
with a pairing.

Proof.

The datum (
198
) follows from restricting
E({\sf L}^{-}_{M})
in Corollary
5.11
along (
193
). The property (
199
) follows from (
163
) in Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Proposition 6.26
.

Given a pairing on a degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
, the Clifford linear superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{+},{\mathbb{A}}_{-}
from Proposition
6.20
satisfy the adjunction formula,

(200)

\displaystyle L({\mathbb{A}}_{-}v,w)+(-1)^{|v|}L(v,{\mathbb{A}}_{+}w)=dL(v,w).

Proof.

Using the descriptions of
{\rm proj}^{*}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}
and
{\rm act}^{*}_{+}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}
in terms of modules over
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}))
and
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}))
(see Lemma
3.20
) and the notation from Proposition
6.20
we compute

\displaystyle({\rm proj}_{+}^{*}L)(({\rm inv}^{*}\rho_{-})(t,\theta,v),w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\rho_{-}(-t,-\theta,v),w)=L((A_{-}(-t)+\theta B_{-}(-t))v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm act}^{*}_{+}L)(v,\rho_{+}(t,\theta,w))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(v,\rho_{+}(t,\theta,w)\rangle+\theta d\langle v,\rho_{+}(t,\theta,w))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(v,(A_{+}(t)-\theta B_{+}(t))w\rangle+\theta d\langle v,(A_{+}(t)-\theta B_{+}(t))w),

where the equalities above are between elements of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}))
.
Collecting the terms involving
\theta
, setting
t=0
, and using the definition of the superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{+}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{-}
, the result follows.
∎

6.5.
Reflection structures and real structures

Using the functors
{\sf R}
and
{\sf Or}
from Lemma
3.22
,
({\sf sPath}_{0}(M),{\sf R}\circ{\sf Or})
is a Real super Lie category in the sense of Definition
6.13
, where
\overline{({\sf R}\circ{\sf Or})}\circ({\sf R}\circ{\sf Or})={{\rm id}}
(and so
\eta
is the identity natural isomorphism).

Definition 6.27
.

A
reflection structure
for the degree
n
twist is a Real structure for the twist relative to
({\sf sPath}_{0}(M),{\sf R}\circ{\sf Or})
.

We refer to §
A.2
for the definition of a
*
-superalgebra.

Lemma 6.28
.

A
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
determines a reflection structure for the degree
n
twist.

Proof.

By Definition
6.14
and Lemma
6.15
, a Real structure is an invertible
\mathscr{T}_{0}-{\sf Or}_{0}^{*}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}_{0}}
-bimodule
over
{\rm Ob}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\simeq{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
with additional involutive data. For the degree
n
twist it suffices to consider the case
M={\rm pt}
. Such an invertible bimodule can be constructed from isomorphisms of algebras

(201)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}=\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}^{\sf{op}}

satisfying an involutive property. Indeed, the above allows us to view
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
as a
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}^{\sf{op}}
-
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
bimodule. An involutive algebra isomorphism (
201
) is precisely the data of a
*
-superalgebra structure, proving the lemma.
∎

Hereafter, we fix a reflection structure for the degree
n
twist from the following
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

Definition 6.29
.

Define a
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
given by the
{\mathbb{C}}
-antilinear extension of the map on generators,

(202)

\displaystyle f_{j}^{*}=-if_{j},\qquad e_{j}^{*}=ie_{j}.

using the notation of generators for the Clifford algebra from (
2.4
).

Definition 6.30
.

Suppose we are given a degree
n
representation
E
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
with a pairing
L
. For the reflection structure for the degree
n
twist fixed by (
202
), define a
reflection structure
on
(E,L)
as a Real twisted representation (see Definition
6.14
) with the additional property that the the pairing,

\langle-,-\rangle\colon{\sf Or}_{0}^{*}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}}_{+}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\sf Or}_{0}^{*}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{+}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+})\xrightarrow{L}\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

is hermitian in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded sense, where the second isomorphism above makes use of the Real structure on the representation. A degree
n
representation with a reflection structure is
reflection positive
if the above pairing restricts along
M\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
to a positive pairing (again in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded sense).

Proposition 6.31
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the restriction of a reflection structure on a degree
n
,
1|1
-Euclidean field theory along (
32
) determines a reflection structure on a degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
. The restriction of a degree
n
reflection positive
1|1
-Euclidean field theory along (
32
) determines a degree
n
reflection positive representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
.

Proof.

Hypothesis
5.9
states that the reflection structure for the degree
n
twist
\mathscr{T}^{\otimes n}\colon 1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{Alg}}
comes from the
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
. If we then further examine the data in Proposition
5.4
and Corollary
5.11
, the restriction of a reflection structure along (
193
) evidentially determines a Real degree
n
representation for the real structure on
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
given by
{\sf R}\circ{\sf Or}
. The symmetry of the pairing in Definition
6.30
follows from Lemma
5.14
. Finally, we observe that the positivity condition in Definition
5.16
is equivalent to the positivity condition in Definition
6.30
.
∎

Proposition 6.32
.

The
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-linear superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}_{+}
associated with a degree
n
reflection positive representation is self-adjoint with respect to the pairing from Definition
6.30
.

Proof.

A reflection structure gives isomorphisms of vector bundles over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
,

(203)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{\mp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\sf Or}_{0}^{*}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\pm}.

Relative to these isomorphisms, we have the identities

\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta)=({\sf or}^{*}{\sf r}^{*}\overline{\rho}_{\mp})(t,\theta)=i^{\rm deg}\overline{\rho}_{\mp}(-t,i\theta).

Taylor expanding the above expression (as in (
196
)), we find that the superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}_{-}
is sent to
-i^{{\rm deg}+1}\overline{{\mathbb{A}}_{+}}
under the isomorphism (
203
). Then by the adjunction formula (
200
) and the definition of the hermitian pairing (
189
), we obtain the desired self-adjointness property for
{\mathbb{A}}_{+}
.
∎

We may also consider Real degree
n
representations of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
for the Real structure given by
{\sf R}
. This leads to a discussion analogous to the one for reflection structures.

Lemma 6.33
.

For the Real super Lie category
({\sf sPath}_{0}(M),{\sf R})
, a Real structure for the degree
n
twist is determined by a real structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
.

Definition 6.34
.

Define an antilinear involution
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\to\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{n}
by declaring the generators of the Clifford algebra in (
2.4
) to be real, and take the extension to a
{\mathbb{C}}
-antilinear map.

Definition 6.35
.

Suppose we are given a degree
n
representation
E
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
with a pairing
L
. For the Real structure for the degree
n
twist fixed by Definition
6.34
, define a
real, reflection positive degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
as a Real structure on
E
relative to
{\sf R}
(in the sense of Definition
6.14
), a reflection structure on
E
satisfying the reflection positivity condition, and the further condition that the pairing satisfies the reality condition

(204)

\displaystyle\langle x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|\overline{x}|}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{\langle\overline{x},\overline{y}\rangle}

for
x,y\in\Gamma(V_{+})
, and
\overline{x},\overline{y}
their image under the isomorphism
V_{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf R}_{0}^{*}\overline{V}_{+}
.

Proposition 6.36
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the restriction of a real, reflection positive, degree
n
,
1|1
-Euclidean field theory along (
32
) determines a real, reflection positive degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
.

Proof.

The reality data on the degree
n
representation
E
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
is clear. It remains to check the condition on the pairing. This follows from the argument in the proof of
[
HST10
, Theorem 6.48]
, where the relation between the reflection and real structures in (
91
) introduces the sign
(-1)^{|\overline{x}|}
.
∎

Proposition 6.37
.

A real, reflection positive degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
equips the vector bundle
V=V_{+}
with a real structure such that the pairing (
204
) comes from an ordinary (i.e., not
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded) real pairing on the underlying real vector bundle and the self-adjoint superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}={\mathbb{A}}_{+}
is a real superconnection.

Proof.

A Real structure for a degree
n
representation with respect to the involution
{\sf R}
gives isomorphisms of bundles of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-modules over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
,

(205)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\sf R}^{*}_{0}\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\pm},

relative to the chosen real structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
. This gives an ordinary real structure on
V_{+},V_{-}\to M
. The super semigroup representations satisfy the identity

(206)

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}(t,\theta)=({\sf R}^{*}_{1}\overline{\rho}_{\pm})(t,\theta).

Taylor expanding the above expression (as in (
196
)), the associated superconnections are real. The condition (
204
) implies that the pairing is real for pairings between even sections, zero for pairings between even and odd sections, and purely imaginary for pairings between odd sections. The translation between a super Hilbert space and a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded Hilbert space is (compare
[
DEF+99
, page 91]
)

\displaystyle(v,w):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle v,w\rangle&v,w\in\Gamma(V_{+})^{\rm ev}\\
i^{-1}\langle v,w\rangle&v,w\in\Gamma(V_{+})^{\rm odd}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.

and so we see that the condition (
204
) leads to a real pairing (
6.5
) in the usual sense.
∎

6.6.
Trace class representations

We recall the super Euclidean loop space
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)
from Definition
3.26
with its action by
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
. We observe that the loop space and its equivariant structure are natural in the manifold
M
, and hence we have a map of stacks

(211)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)\to\mathcal{L}^{1|1}({\rm pt})/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)={\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2).

Definition 6.38
.

The
Pfaffian line bundle

{\sf{Pf}}\to\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
is the pullback along (
211
) of the odd line bundle classified by

{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)\xrightarrow{p}{\rm pt}/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/2={\rm pt}/\!\!/\{\pm 1\}\to{\rm pt}/\!\!/{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

where
p
is determined by the projections
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\to{\rm pt}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2\to{\mathbb{Z}}/2
, and we take the standard
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-action on
\Pi{\mathbb{C}}
, the trivial odd line bundle over
{\rm pt}
. The Pfaffian line bundle is natural for maps of stacks
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)\to\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(N)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)
induced by maps of smooth manifolds
M\to N
.

Remark 6.39
.

Identify the quotient stack
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}/\!\!/{\mathbb{R}}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
with the stack of metrized circles with periodic spin structure, where
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
acts by the spin flip on the spinor bundle, and
{\mathbb{R}}
acts by rotation of the circle. The Dirac operator on the circle for the chosen spin structure gives an
{\mathbb{R}}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariant family of operators over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. The terminology in Definition
6.38
comes from the fact that the restriction of
{\sf{Pf}}
along the canonical inclusion
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}/\!\!/{\mathbb{R}}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}/\!\!/{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
is the Pfaffian line bundle of the family of Dirac operators. This Pfaffian line bundle appears in Stolz and Teichner’s sketch of the degree
n
twist for the Euclidean bordism category without supersymmetry; see
[
ST11
, §5.3]
.

Proposition 6.40
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes n}
denote the
n
th tensor power of the Pfaffian line bundle over
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)
. Sections are in bijection with

\Gamma(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2);{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes n})\simeq\left\{Z,Z_{\ell}\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))\mid\begin{array}[]{c}dZ=0,\ \partial_{\ell}Z=dZ_{\ell}\\
{\rm deg}(Z)=n\mod 2,\\
{\rm deg}(Z_{\ell})=n-1\mod 2\end{array}\right\}.

Proof.

Under the identification

C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}))

sections of
{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes n}
can be identified with a differential form valued in
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0})
with equivariance properties for the action by
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2
. Choosing coordinates
(\ell,\lambda)
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{>0}
and Taylor expanding a section
s
in the odd variable
\lambda
, consider the parameterization

s=\ell^{{\rm deg}/2}Z+2i\lambda\ell^{({\rm deg}+1)/2}Z_{\ell},\qquad Z,Z_{\ell}\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})).

By
[
BE23a
, Proposition 1.4]
,
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
-invariance is equivalent to the conditions
dZ=0
and
\partial_{\ell}Z=dZ_{\ell}
. The degree condition on
(Z,Z_{\ell})
comes from equivariance for the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action which acts by
(-1)^{\rm deg}
on differential forms and
(\ell,\lambda)\mapsto(\ell,\pm\lambda)
. The result follows.
∎

Define the element
\Gamma_{n}
of the
n
th Clifford algebra

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{-n/2}f_{1}f_{2}\dots f_{n}&n\geq 0\\
2^{n/2}e_{1}e_{2}\dots e_{n}&n<0\end{array}\right.

in the notation from (
2.4
).
The
Clifford super trace
of a
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-linear endomorphism
T\colon V\to V
is

{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(T):={\sf sTr}(\Gamma_{n}\circ T),

see §
A.4
below.

Definition 6.41
.

A
trace class representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
of degree
n
is the data of a degree
n
representation
E
of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
and a section
(Z(E),Z(E)_{\ell})\in\Gamma(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}_{0}(M);{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes-n})
satisfying the property

(215)

Z(E)=\ell^{-{\rm deg}/2}{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\rho_{+}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)})\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))

where
{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}
denotes the Clifford super trace.

Lemma 6.42
.

For a trace class degree
n
representation,
Z(E)\in\Omega_{\rm cl}^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))
is a closed differential form in degrees equal to the parity of
n
(i.e., either even or odd degree). When
E
is a real field theory,
Z(E)
is a real differential form. When
E
is real and reflection positive,
Z(E)
is real and concentrated in degrees equal to
n
mod 4.

Proof.

For the first, note that the element
\Gamma_{n}\in{\rm Cl}_{n}\subset{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
has parity equal to the parity of
n
, and so the endomorphism
\Gamma_{n}\circ\rho_{+}
also has parity
n
mod 2. Hence, it follows for any degree
n
representation for which the trace (
215
) is defined, the resulting function is even or odd according to this parity. The real structure preserves
\Gamma_{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
, and the reality condition (
206
) implies that
Z(E)
be equal to its conjugate. Since
\Gamma_{n}\in{\rm Cl}_{n}
determines a real trivialization (see (
6.6
)), this amounts to demanding that
Z(E)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))
is real as a differential form, for the standard conjugation action on
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued differential forms. When
E
is also reflection positive, we observe that
\Gamma_{n}\mapsto i^{n}\Gamma_{n}
under the
*
-superalgebra structure (
202
). This imposes an additional invariance property on
Z(E)
, implying that it takes values in 4-periodic differential forms.
∎

Lemma 6.43
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, a choice of orientation on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
determines an isomorphism
{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes n}\simeq\mathscr{T}^{\otimes n}(\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2))
between the Pfaffian line bundle and the valued of the degree
n
twist on
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2)
. In particular, a degree
n
field theory determines a section of the dual of
{\sf{Pf}}^{\otimes n}
.

Proof.

It suffices to take
M={\rm pt}
. Then the value of the twist on
\mathcal{L}^{1|1}({\rm pt})
is the line bundle with fiber

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}/[{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n},{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}].

A choice of orientation
\Gamma_{n}\in\Lambda^{\rm top}({\mathbb{C}}^{n})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}/[{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n},{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}]
trivializes this line, and so after this choice it remains to compute the equivariance structure. Under Hypothesis
5.9
, the
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
-action is trivial, and the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action is by parity. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.44
.

Assuming Hypothesis
5.9
, the restriction of a degree
n
field theory along (
32
) determines a trace class degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to
[
ST11
, Lemma 3.20]
. In the terminology of
[
ST12
]
, the morphisms in the image of the composition

(216)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\hookrightarrow{\rm Mor}({\sf sPath}_{0}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

are
thick
: this follows from Proposition
4.15
. The categorical trace of a thick morphism is an endomorphism of the monoidal unit. Proposition
4.18
shows that this categorical trace is the image of the composition

{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to\mathcal{L}_{0}^{1|1}(M)\to{\rm Mor}(1|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)).

Applying a degree
n
field theory to these families, Corollary
5.11
shows that the categorical trace of the family (
216
) is sent to the composition

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}\otimes R_{t,0}^{+}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{+}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{-})\xrightarrow{\sigma}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\mathcal{V}}_{-}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{+})\xrightarrow{L_{0}}\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}.

The element
\Gamma_{n}
provides an identification
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\simeq\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
with the trivial line bundle, and the above composition becomes the Clifford super trace. Finally, using that nuclear Fréchet spaces have the approximation property
[
Sch71
, page 109]
, we find that the categorical trace above is independent of the choice of thickener
[
ST12
, Theorem 1.7]
, completing the proof.
∎

7.
Energy cutoffs and the families index

In this section we prove Theorem
1.1
via an index bundle construction applied to the self-adjoint superconnections extracted from field theories in Proposition
1.10
. The general index bundle construction for a superconnection is reviewed in §
C
. When comparing with objects from index theory, we refer to §
A
for the translation between the super sign conventions in the previous sections with the standard sign conventions from differential geometry for
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded inner products, self-adjoint superconnections, and Clifford actions.

7.1.
Energy cutoffs

Definition 7.1
.

Let
E
denote a degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
, and let
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
denote the superconnections afforded by Lemma
4.24
. Then
E
is
infinitesimally generated
if we have the equality of differential forms

(217)

\displaystyle{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\rho_{+}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)})={\sf sTr}_{{\rm Cl}_{n}}(\exp(-t{\mathbb{A}}^{2}))\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})),

compare (
215
).
An infinitesimally generated representation
admits smooth energy cutoffs
if furthermore the superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\pm}
admits smooth index bundles in the sense of Definition
C.16
.

Remark 7.2
.

We comment on the conditions imposed in Definition
7.1
. Although one expects a field theory to be determined by infinitesimal data (implying the condition (
217
)), this is not automatic in the formalism
[
ST11
]
due to the failure of existence and uniqueness of solutions to differential equations in topological vector spaces; see Remark
1.5
.
The existence of smooth index bundles is more subtle. For a family of Dirac operators, the existence of an index bundle follows from ellipticity, using that eigensections of the Dirac Laplacian are smooth, e.g., see
[
BGV92
, Proposition 9.10]
or
[
LM94
, III, Theorem 5.8]
. As such, the existence of smooth cutoffs can be thought of as an ellipticity property for field theories. In higher dimensions, one can formulate similar smooth cutoff conditions for subspaces
\mathcal{H}^{\vec{\lambda}}\subset\mathcal{H}
indexed by both energies and momenta, with the number of cutoff indices equal to the dimension of the field theory.

Definition
7.1
is the final ingredient in the groupoid
1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
, see Definition
1.11
. For simplicity, we will often refer to an object as
E\in 1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
, emphasizing the degree
n
representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
and suppressing the remaining data. The simplest examples of objects in
1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
come from superconnections on finite rank bundles.

The following is the evident degree
n
generalization of Proposition
1.4
.

Proposition 7.3
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle{\rm sPar}\colon{\sf Vect}^{{\mathbb{A}}}_{{\rm Cl}_{n},{\rm ind}}(M)\xrightarrow{\sim}1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)

from the category of real,
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear self-adjoint superconnections admitting smooth index bundles to the category
1|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
.

Proof.

For a
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}
on a real super vector bundle
V\to M
, consider the representation of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
defined on the vector bundles with sections

\Gamma(\mathcal{V}_{+}):=\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\mathbb{C}}),\qquad\Gamma(\mathcal{V}_{-}):=\Omega^{\bullet}(M;\overline{V}_{\mathbb{C}})

and super semigroup actions given by

(218)

\displaystyle e^{-t{\mathbb{A}}^{2}+\theta{\mathbb{A}}}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\to{\rm End}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;V_{\mathbb{C}})),\qquad e^{-t\overline{{\mathbb{A}}}^{2}+\theta\overline{{\mathbb{A}}}}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\leq 0}\to{\rm End}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;\overline{V}_{\mathbb{C}})).

A pairing for this representation is inherited from the hermitian pairing
\overline{V}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\mathbb{C}}\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
built in the usual way from the underlying metric on
V
. The real and reflection structures are evident from the construction, as is the reflection positivity condition. The trace structure uses that
\exp(-t{\mathbb{A}}^{2})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\rm End}(V_{\mathbb{C}}))
is trace class (from the existence of smooth index bundles), and
Z_{\ell}
is determined by the Chern–Simons form for the 1-parameter family of superconnections gotten from Getzler rescaling of
{\mathbb{A}}
. Existence of cutoffs follows by assumption. This constructs the functor
{\rm sPar}
in (
28
) on objects. The construction on morphisms is straightforward: using
218
, isomorphisms between superconnections are sent to isomorphisms between representations of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
. The functor is full and faithful since isomorphisms of representations of
{\sf sPath}_{0}(M)
are determined by isomorphisms of vector bundles. Finally, Proposition
1.10
verifies that
{\rm sPar}
is essentially surjective, and hence an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

The groupoid of self-adjoint
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnections on finite-rank real vector bundles admits a functor
{\rm sPar}

(219)

\displaystyle\hbox to183.92pt{\vbox to21.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 38.93439pt\lower-8.96968pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} ;
